<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, December 13, 2016  
PRESENT:  Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; Steve Williams, Supervisor; Jesse Thompson, Vice-Chairman; 
and Jason E. Whiting, Chairman, were present in person. Robert K. Black, Jr., Supervisor was absent.  
9:03 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation was led by Supervisor Williams.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no comments from the public. 
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 November 2016  b) Minutes: November 22, 
2016 c) Constable Reports: Snowflake Precinct #3, October and Show Low Precinct #5, November 2016 d) Clerk 
of Superior Court Report October and November 2016  e) Juvenile Probation Report November 2016  f) Justice 
Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1,  November; Winslow Precinct #2, November; Snowflake Precinct #3, 
November; Kayenta Precinct #4, November; Show Low Pr ecinct #5, November; and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct 
#6, November 2016  g) Reissuance of Stale Dated Check  h) Extension to the existing Memorandum of 
Understanding with the Navajo Nation Division of Transportation  i) Amendment Number One to the 
Intergovernmental Agreement with the State of Arizona Department of Transportation for the 
Woodruff/Snowflake Bridge to reduce the County’s costs:  Supervisor Thompson made a motion to authorize 
the Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda; motion seconded by  Supervisor Whitesinger . Vote 
unanimous approving the motion  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Recognition for the Hopi High School Cross Country Team for winning their 27 th consecutive State 
Championship: Adam Wolfe indicated that this is the longest cross country streak in the country.  He showed 
a video which was aired on ESPN.  Coach Baker addressed the Board regarding the team, the season and the 
athletes. Coach Baker introduced the Athletic Director, Mr. Greer; Mr. & Mrs. Dennis, parents of one of the 
athletes; and the bus driver. Supervisor Thompson spoke to the athletes. He spoke about Hopi and the spirit 
within each of them. He spoke about their future and their continued education. He encouraged them to never 
stop and to keep running now and in life. Supervisor Thompson  presented each athlete with a medal. 
Supervisor Williams stated that the video has inspired his son, who runs cross country in Show Low.  He 
thanked the Hopi Cross Country team for inspiring not only their community but others, as well . He stated 
that Coach Baker is a superstar. Supervisor Whitesinger spoke to the athletes, thanking them for allowing 
us to recognize them. She spoke about the dedication that it takes to do what they have done and the 
responsibility to their team. Chairman Whiting spoke to the athletes an d thanked them for their spirit and 
indicated that they are an inspiration.  
b. Recognition of Supervisor Robert K. Black Jr. : Chairman Whiting indicated that Supervisor Black was 
unable to be present today; however, we thank him for his service to Navajo County.  
c. Presentation to Navajo County for the “Most Creative Float” during the December 3rd Holbrook Parade of 
Lights: Adam Wolfe indicated that Navajo County won an award for “Most Creative Float” and presented the 
plaque to Raecine John, Jayla Johnson, Ryan Taylor, Bryan Cook, Brandon Andrews and Danny Shurter  
SUPERIOR COURT: Informational presentation by the Navajo County Superior Court Veteran’s Court team 
and the first graduate: Brad Carlyon indicated that Judge Dale Nielson is in the middle of a jury trial and that the 
first graduate that they wanted to highlight was unable to be present. Mr. Carlyon asked that this item be moved 
to the second meeting in January.

<!-- page 2 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT : Board of Directors Session:  Supervisor 
Williams made a motion  to enter into the Navajo County Public Health Services District Board of Directors 
Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. CONSENT AGENDA: Professional Services Contract 
with Debra Smalley, RNP, CNM for public health nurse services to fill contract requirements for Family Planning, 
and STD grants with the Arizona Department of Health Services for January 1, 2017 to December 31, 2019 at the 
rate of $2,0 00.00 per month : Daniel Duschon discussed the item.  Supervisor Williams  made a motion  to 
approve the professional services contract with Debra Smalley, RNP, CNM for public health nurse services to fill 
contract requirements for Family Planning and STD gra nts with the Arizona Department of Health Services for 
January 1, 2017 to December 31, 2019 at the rate of $2,000.00 per month ; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
Supervisor Williams made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
SHERIFF’S OFFICE: Consideration and possible approval of the Data Sharing Agreement between Navajo 
County Sheriff’s Office and Arizona Board of Regents for and on behalf of Arizona State University: Chief James 
Molesa discussed the item. Supervisor Williams made a motion  to approve  the Data Sharing Agreement 
between Navajo County Sheriff’s Office and Arizona Board of Regents for and on behalf of Arizona State 
University; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions: Katherine Wright reviewed the 37-item personnel 
action report. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the Personnel Action Report as presented; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval to rescind the contract for professional services with Doyel Shaml ey. 
Veritas Research Consulting, LTD: Mary Springer discussed the item indicating that Doyel Shamley recently 
won his election as District III Supervisor for Apache County and that there are competitive grants which both 
counties would be going after and there would be a conflict. She spoke about the submission of a grant award 
with State Forestry for $60,000.00 . Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion  to approve  rescinding the 
contract for professional services with Doyel Shamley, Veritas Research Consulting, LTD; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Chairman Whiting thanked Ms. Springer 
for the wonderful job that she has done.  
c. Consideration and possible approval of the current Navajo County Hazardous Materials Response Plan: Mary 
Springer discussed the item indicating that there has been some minor changes to the plan.  She requested that 
the Board approve the updated plan . Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the current Navajo 
County Hazardous Materials Response Plan; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
PUBLIC WORKS: Consideration and possible approval of Task Agreement Number 6 with the Bureau of Indian 
Affairs (BIA) to provide drainage pipes to BIA: Glenn Kephart discussed the item. Supervisor Thompson made 
a motion to approve Task Agreement Number 6 with the Bureau of Indian Affairs (BIA) to provide drainage 
pipes to BIA; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY RECREATION IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT:  Board of 
Directors Session:  Supervisor Williams made a motion to enter into the Whi te Mountain Lakes County 
Recreation Improvement District Board of Directors session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger.  Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 3 -->
a. Consideration and possible approval to add to the Rules and Regulations under Section 14.0 Pets, a subsection 
"14.3 No horses or horse trailers are allowed in the parking area or on the beach":  Melissa Buckley discussed 
the item.  Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve the addition to the Rules and Regulations 
under Section 14.0 Pets, a subsection “14.3 No horses or horse trailers are allowed in the parking area or on 
the beach”; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson.  Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
b. Consideration and possible acceptance of the 3 rd Party Audit: Ms. Buckley provided the Board with a draft 
copy of the 3rd party audit.  Brandt Clark gave a brief history as to why the 3rd party audit took place, including 
the fact that there has been a significant amount of turnover in the chairmanship and membership of the 
Advisory Committee. He stated that the previous chairman, Jerry McGraw, requested that this financial audit 
be done, but the audit took longer than Chairman McGraw’s tenure. He spoke about the results of that audit. 
He indicated that the audit was not as bad as we had thought due to the fact that several years ago, the County 
took over a lot of the payments. He spoke about the issues that need to be rectified, including there being no 
accounting for the cash transactions; a separate bank account with Bank of the West , which the audit 
recommends closing; a Christmas bonus given to employees , which showed the lack of und erstanding and 
training on how we distribute public monies ; and the current use of a payroll service, ADP, which the 
recommendation is to bring that in-house to the finance department. Mr. Clark recommended that the Board 
accept the audit and move forward with the recommended safeguards.  Chairman Whiting asked Mr. Clark 
about the things that we should be concerned about from a legal perspective. Mr. Clark spoke about the lack 
of accounting for the cash on hand. Mr. Clark stated that he has some very large concerns about the White 
Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District . He said that the District is very lucky to have 
Melissa Buckley involved in it. He spoke about an open meeting law complaint on which Ms. Buckley spent 
tireless hours, including time away from her family , to make sure that the District was not found to be in 
violation of open meeting law violations .  He stated that moving forward, he looks to the continuity of the 
Advisory Committee.  He spoke about the ambiguity with regard to employees of the District and to whom 
they report.  He stated that the Advisory Committee meets on a far too frequent basis, which creates public 
documents and records and requires public notice, and not much is being accomplished at these meetings.  He 
indicated that the Advisory Committee should meet for rule changes, etc., not for purchasing a lock for a gate.  
He stated there is always the chance for litigation that can come up.  He stated that moving forward, there 
needs to be more oversight and transparency . Chairman Whiting thanked Mr. Clark and Ms. Buckley for 
the work they both have done. He spoke about how what happens with the District comes back to the Board 
of Supervisors. Mr. Clark thanked Ms. Buckley for her work  and the great resource she has been for the 
District. Supervisor Williams made a motion to accept the 3rd party audit; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Mr. Jayne thanked Mr. Clark for his help with this 
situation.  
Supervisor Williams made a motion to move out of the White Mountain Lakes Recreation Improvement 
District Board of Directors session and move into Executive Session; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.    
COUNTY ATTORNEY:  
a. At 10:05 a.m. the Board of Supervisors entered into EXECUTIVE SESSION pursuant to A.R.S. § 38 -
431.03(A) (4) for the Board to receive legal advi ce from its attorney regarding possible action of pending 
litigation.  
At 10:20 a.m. Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to return to regular session ; motion seconded by  
Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. RECONVENE:  Consideration and possible authorization to enter into settlement negotiations: Supervisor 
Williams made a motion to authorize the County Attorney’s Office to enter into settlement negotiations with

<!-- page 4 -->
an amount not to exceed the amount discussed in executive session; moti on seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager :  
Supervisor Whitesinger spoke about Safer Streets and expressed appreciation to all those pa rticipating in that 
conversation and Nolan from the White Mountain Independent  for getting the word out .  She spoke about the 
Arizona Conservation Corps and the projects in which the students participated.  She stated that there is one 
mountain open at Sunrise Park Resort, and if you go on Christmas Eve dressed as Santa, the Grinch or a similar 
character, you can ski for free that day.  She wished everyone a Merry Christmas.  Supervisor Williams reported 
on the Show Low Community Fast and indicated that a little over $12,000.00 was raised, and every penny goes 
to help local families in the area.  He wished everyone a Merry Christmas.  Supervisor Thompson spoke about 
attending AACo and visiting the senior programs.  He spoke about working with Public Works to review ongoing 
projects and other projects to be done and how to obtain additional revenues to move forward with proposed 
projects.  Brad Carlyon wished everyone a Merry Christmas.  Chairman Whiting indicated that he was able to 
meet with ATRA and thanked Mr. Carylon and Mr. Layton.  He spoke about identifying areas on which we have 
common ground and work with the legislature on those issues.  He stated that he met with the Senate regarding 
the coal power plants and its effect on our area.  He spoke about being approached by several senators who 
expressed appreciation for the local perspective.  He stated that there have been several great events throughout 
the County over the last several days.  He spoke about reaching out to those who stand in need.  He w ished 
everyone a Merry Christmas. 
 
ADJOURN: At 10:28 Supervisor Williams made a motion to adjourn ; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
 
11:00 a.m. to 1:00 p.m. NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS AND ADMINISTRATIVE 
SERVICES CHRISTMAS LUNCHEON: Pursuant to ARS § 38-431.02 and ARS § 38 -431 (3), notice is 
hereby given to the members of the Navajo County Board of Supervisors and to the General Public that a 
quorum of the Board of Supervisors may be present for Christmas luncheon at the Celebrations of 
Generations, 432 S. Main Street, Snowflake, Arizona where no official action will be taken.  
 
APPROVED:       DATED:  January 10, 2016 
 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
